[{'type': 'input_text', 'text': 'Script completed\nWall time 0.9 seconds\nOutput:\n'}, {'type': 'input_text', 'text': 'Warning: truncated output (original token count: 45906)\nTotal output lines: 1\n\n{"chunk_id":"25303e","wall_time_seconds":0.6981868,"exit_code":0,"original_token_count":76930,"output":"Warning: truncated output (original token count: 76930)\\nTotal output lines: 3961\\n\\n\\n### FILE: C:\\\\Users\\\\Joshu\\\\dev\\\\ai-os\\\\raw\\\\sources\\\\old-obsidian-vault-2026-10-07\\\\Clippings\\\\loophole-online\\\\routines\\\\Translation Foundation Routine • LO.md\\n\\r\\n---\\ntitle: \\"Translation Foundation Routine • LO\\"\\nsource: \\"https://new.theloophole.com/workouts-and-routines/routines/translation-foundation-routine\\"\\nauthor:\\npublished:\\ncreated: 2026-08-30\\ndescription: \\"LSAT prep from Ellen Cassidy, author of The Loophole in LSAT Logical Reasoning — video lessons, drills, and guided study plans.\\"\\ntags:\\n  - \\"clippings\\"\\n---\\n## Quick Facts\\n\\n### Will This Help Me?\\n\\nThis will help you if\\n\\nYou\'re just starting out in your Translation skill building.\\n\\nSkip for now if\\n\\nDon\'t skip this Routine.\\n\\nchallenge indexPretty Chill\\n\\nlevelBeginner\\n\\nfrequencyDaily Foundation\\n\\n![calendar](https://new.theloophole.com/_next/static/media/calendar.778ddbbe.svg)\\n\\nstudy block length2-4 hours\\n\\n## Schedule (4 workouts)\\n\\nEnsure you complete the workouts **in order** to benefit the most from this routine.\\n\\n1\\n\\n### Translation + CLIR Drill\\n\\n≥ 3 sections\\n\\n2\\n\\n### Extracurricular Reading\\n\\n#### Instructions\\n\\n1. Every day, translate elevated reading material from outside the LSAT, using the following sources:\\n\\t1. **[ScienceDaily.com](https://www.sciencedaily.com/)**\\n\\t\\t1. Ideal daily reading for those who feel alienated by scientific language or fear science passages in RC\\n\\t\\t\\t\\t2. Be sure to choose the articles that challenge you the most, not articles that look familiar or interesting\\n\\t\\t2. **[Aeon.co](https://aeon.co/)**\\n\\t\\t1. The most similar articles to LSAT RC on the internet\\n\\t\\t\\t\\t2. Reading an Aeon.co article every day is a great habit if you have no specific topic-based weaknesses, but would like to make RC-style reading a habit.\\n\\t\\t3. **[ALDaily.com](https://www.aldaily.com/)**\\n\\t\\t1. Ideal daily reading for those who feel alienated by abstract language, art, humanities, and pretension.\\n\\t\\t\\t\\t2. Be sure to choose the articles that look the most unfamiliar, not articles that speak to your pre-existing interests.\\n\\t\\t4. **[NYT Guide to Essential Knowledge](https://www.amazon.com/York-Times-Guide-Essential-Knowledge-ebook/dp/B005EXS7E4/ref=tmm_kin_swatch_0)**\\n\\t\\t1. The single best way to fix gaps in common knowledge\\n\\t\\t\\t\\t2. Here is a list of sections in the Guide that are relevant to LSAT. If any of these topics would trouble you as an RC passage, read over that section of the guide.\\n\\t\\t\\t\\t3. **LSAT Relevant Guide Sections**: Architecture, Zoology, Botany, History, Art, Photography, Literature, Music (the serious parts about jazz and blues, especially), Film, Dance, Drama, Climate & Weather and Glossary of Weather Words (In Geography), Government, Law, Philosophy, Biology, Chemistry, Physics, Astronomy, Geology\\n\\t\\t5. **[Crash Course](https://www.youtube.com/@crashcourse/playlists)**\\n\\t\\t1. Not a written source, but Crash Course is too useful for solving common knowledge gaps to not include\\n\\t\\t\\t\\t2. Watch Crash Course while you\'re in line, waiting for someone, on hold, brushing your teeth, etc. Any time you could be scrolling on your phone, watch Crash Course instead.\\n\\t\\t\\t\\t3. **Top Five LSAT Relevant Crash Course Sections**: Intellectual Property (Ellen\'s favorite for LSAT students), Economics, US Government & Politics, Art History, and Philosophy\\n\\n3\\n\\n### Vocab Training\\n\\n### Once A Week, Do This Instead\\n\\n1\\n\\n### Translation Marathon\\n\\n8 sections\\n\\n≥ 1x per week\\n\\n## Ellen\'s Advice\\n\\n### Why Are We Doing This?\\n\\nThe Translation + CLIR Drills in this Routine are the foundation of your entire LSAT prep journey. This is the most important homework you will do during your LSAT prep training, even compared to PTs.\\n\\nThis Routine builds a foundation in LR Translation before we port your Translation skills over to RC in the next Routine.\\n\\n### How This Should Be Done\\n\\nYour progression toward the Translation time and accuracy goals is primarily determined by two things: **volume** and **following the instructions.**\\n\\nTo maximize your progress in this Routine, do all your Translation + CLIR Drills consecutively; do not take breaks between Translation + CLIR Drills. Do not review your Translation + CLIR Drills in between sections. All review should be done in a separate sitting.\\n\\n**Do at least 3 consecutive Translation + CLIR Drills per day, if possible.** Ideally, you would complete 6 Translation + CLIR drills consecutively per day. Doing 1 Translation + CLIR Drill per day will not improve your skills; it will keep your skills the same. You will only start improving on your second consecutive Translation + CLIR Drill; this is why it is so useful to do *at least* 3 consecutive Translation + CLIR Drills every day.\\n\\nAdd a Translation Marathon to your Daily Practice at least once per week. If you can manage to do Translation + CLIR Marathons more often, you will progress to the time goal faster.\\n\\nExtracurricular Reading and Vocab should always be done at the end of your Routine to gradually expand your common sense in the subject areas where you\'re weakest. If you struggle with science passages, emphasize daily science engagement in the Extracurricular Reading. If you struggle with abstract language, emphasize daily humanities engagement in the Extracurricular Reading.\\n\\n### How to Move On\\n\\n**Once you are halfway to your Translation + CLIR Drill time goal, move to the next Routine: Translation + Decaf.**\\n\\n**The time goal for Translation + CLIR Drills is 20 minutes.** The accommodated time goals for Translation + CLIR Drills are 25 minutes for 53-minute accommodations and 30 minutes for 70-minute accommodations. Meeting this Translation + CLIR Drill time goal is only valid under two conditions: you have a CLIR for every stimulus and you have translated and quipped every stimulus in the section out loud. Ideally, you should hit -5 accuracy against the CLIR Answer Key consistently in order to beat the time goal, but this accuracy goal may be adjusted according to your score goals.\\n\\n**Example of When to Move On**: You started at 52 minutes per out loud Translation + CLIR Drill section and you don\'t have accommodations, so your time goal is 20 minutes. The midpoint of 52 and 20 is 36, so you would move on when your time hits 36 minutes.\\r\\n\\n### FILE: C:\\\\Users\\\\Joshu\\\\dev\\\\ai-os\\\\raw\\\\sources\\\\old-obsidian-vault-2026-10-07\\\\Clippings\\\\loophole-online\\\\training-camp\\\\accuracy\\\\How to Camo • LO.md\\n\\r\\n---\\ntitle: \\"How to Camo • LO\\"\\nsource: \\"https://new.theloophole.com/training-camp/browse/stage/accuracy/video/how-to-camo\\"\\nauthor:\\npublished:\\ncreated: 2026-08-30\\ndescription: \\"LSAT prep from Ellen Cassidy, author of The Loophole in LSAT Logical Reasoning — video lessons, drills, and guided study plans.\\"\\ntags:\\n  - \\"clippings\\"\\n---\\n## How to Camo\\n\\n0:14\\n\\n/\\n\\n8:25\\n\\n- 0:00How to Camo so I comment on Camo in other videos But I just want to have one dedicated video in case anyone\'s bopping around and they were just interested to learn about Camo solo independent of any other topic\\n- 0:11and so Camo what is it? it is a way to review your sections in which you put in all the answers that you chose in a section and Camo will generate a list of questions for you to return to that includes all of your wrong answers and an unknown number of decoy correct answers\\n- 0:26and so You then have this anonymized list you go back through all of the questions you redo them earnestly And then you place your new answers in Camo you tell it what you pick this time And then it sorts your mistakes into conceptual gaps those are the questions you missed twice you missed both\\n- 0:40in the original take of the Section and in your Camo pass miss reads which are the questions you missed in your original take and you got right in your Camo pass Self-doubt\\n- 0:48which are questions you got right and were decoys and then they they come back wrong in the Camo Changed away from right answer and self-confidence Those are the ones that you got right both times a decoy that comes up that actually you stay with your answer Which is great\\n- 1:02So though that is how you Camo it gives you a lot of really great information because of course Miss reads questions that you were able to correct those are really low-hanging fruit that we want to focus on in terms of like okay What exactly did I do in the moment? Just when I was doing this section\\n- 1:15originally that I can now change that Enabled me to get it right in camera you have a lot more clues to go on because you actually did get it right in Camo so like what did you do differently?\\n- 1:24And then you have your conceptual gaps These are the ones that you got wrong in Camo again either by choosing the same answer or choosing a different answer Those are our more sticky ones and ones that we\'ll need to review more fully Maybe put on The Island to come back to later But they also\\n- 1:38they give us a different sort of clue than the miss reads of okay These are the ones that I genuinely there\'s like something missing that I don\'t understand and we need to resolve it over time\\n- 1:46A self-doubt that\'s where you know you got it right and changed wrong these indicate an issue With second-guessing yourself obviously self-doubt that\'s the name of it\\n- 1:55And it means that you need to work on using your gut instinct and not questioning yourself going with your gut and not Staying around to undo your good thoughts\\n- 2:05and so those are the basics of just what Camo is and how you go about it now Let me give you the kind of finer points to enable you to Camo really really well because\\n- 2:12there are a few things that people Do with Camo that really mess them up And so first thing is you should wait at least 24 hours After doing a section and before Camoing it\\n- 2:23and why do I say this I say this because when people Camo right after they do A section their score is always worse\\n- 2:29Because they are essentially using Camo as a way to check the section and all they really want is the score\\n- 2:34They don\'t really care as much about actually doing well on the Camo they tend to rush it They tend to just choose the same answers that they chose originally under some kind of weird guise of like My integrity says that I chose this\\n- 2:46and I don\'t want to change you know and cheat or something That that weird thing that people do in camera sometimes It\'s not exclusive to doing the section right after you did it originally,\\n- 2:56but you\'re allowed to change your answer in Camo It\'s not like dishonest to have a new idea And so don\'t just stick with your same answers out of some weird misguided sense of loyalty.\\n- 3:04That\'s not like a real thing And so you should wait at least 24 hours Simply because it will improve your Camo score because you\'re more likely to genuinely redo the questions\\n- 3:14You\'ll have a much more level head when you go about them And of course instead of waiting 24 hours You could also have a Camo day once or twice a week where you extend out greater than 24 hours before you Camo which the The reason for it just kind of compounds of like the longer you are away from\\n- 3:30it the more level-headed the less emotional and the more Insights you will be able to glean from your sections, especially doing them in a bigger batch\\n- 3:37Another Camo pro tip do not do Camo Before you prep for the day before you do all your sections do your Camo as the last thing you do in your prep when I build routines for people of what they\'re gonna do every day\\n- 3:48I always put Camo at the end and they always move it to first because they just want to check their sections and get the scores\\n- 3:53But the reason why I always put it at the end is because I don\'t want you being Reactive to your Camo results and immediately being like oh, I just have a problem with Method\\n- 4:02Okay, now I need to make sure I get all the Methods right which like will literally do nothing to actually improve your Method performance But like sure\\n- 4:08Just stay patient remember the results exist without you knowing the results like you knowing the results and the results existing are not the same thing\\n- 4:16and so When when you\'ve waited to Camo wonderful now make sure that you have not actually checked the section\\n- 4:24Before you Camo so the this is a bit tricky in law hub because with law hub when you press complete section or you do The test in exam mode it will auto check the section for you And it literally reveals the right answers to every question and the score and it\'s really really freaking annoying\\n- 4:39In order to get around this problem what I would like you to do is just open the section and open all the sections You\'re gonna do in different tabs on your browser And then after you\'ve chosen your answers write them down separately so you have a record and then just close the tab We\'ll\\n- 4:53save it. It\'s not as though like you know goes away or anything But then you are not automatically put into that complete section bucket You don\'t have to press complete section and always do your tests in self-paced mode Do not do them in exam mode because exam mode is actually\\n- 5:06not very exam me at all It immediately tells you the score right after which is not what will happen on the actual exam, so we don\'t want that\\n- 5:12So you also when you go back through the sections on law hub sometimes if you are you\'re just browsing around you can accidentally mouse Over the section on like the overall menu area where it\'s like listing all the pts and it\'ll tell you the score there as well So be aware there too\\n- 5:26just because you don\'t want to know the score before you Camo because it would inadvertently tell you how Many decoys there are which like obviously we don\'t really want\\n- 5:33So when you go and do Camo though the reason why I want you to have that distance from it is You know so that we can get better insights all that but because it will incentivize you to actually Redo the question for real\\n- 5:46when you\'re Camoing your goal is to get a minus zero on the Camo I know that seems like I feel like I shouldn\'t have to say that but I definitely do\\n- 5:53All we want out of the Camo is to get them right and so we we have no other Objective\\n- 5:59when we go through we want to do the question from start to finish We still want to translate and CLIR that stimulus.\\n- 6:04We still want to go through the question stem We still want to read and translate all the answers and do our full process\\n- 6:09if we\'re not totally sure of our answer We we could hyper skip it in the Camo and come back We would do our normal question remediation processes that we would do in a real section because we want to get that question Right in Camo.\\n- 6:19That\'s the whole point Very often I will find people only go back to their final two answers Especially if they\'re doing the section right after they initially completed it\\n- 6:27They won\'t actually redo the question or like I said that misguided sense of loyalty thing where it\'s like well I chose this and I don\'t really know that it\'s wrong. So\\n- 6:35I stay with it or whatever This also kind of goes in line with just trying to rush through the questions and go super fast Which we also don\'t want to do\\n- 6:46you can say to yourself, okay if I get seven questions back in Camo They aren\'t all right,\\n- 6:51you know, some of them are gonna be wrong So it would be a bad idea to stay with all of the answers that you chose\\n- 6:56Like game the camel a little bit, you know If you\'ve got seven questions back you\'ve got to change some of them, right? Because\\n- 7:02our goal is to get a minus zero on this Camo and so we want to move toward that with with vigor\\n- 7:07And the last thing is do your Wrong Answer Journal while you\'re Camoing So after you\'ve you know put in all of your Camo stuff and it tells you your conceptual gaps tells you your misreads all that That\'s the perfect time to go Wrong Answer Journaling\\n- 7:19And so at that point it\'s a wonderful time start filling that in right then when the question is fresh\\n- 7:23instead of putting it off You really want a Wrong Answer Journal as you go rather than Wrong Answer Journaling in one big bash\\n- 7:30if you start And by as you go I mean as you Camo rather than putting off a Wrong Answer Journaling into a separate sitting because if you allow Wrong Answer Journaling to pile up as like a Big task what happens is it just doesn\'t happen and then you Wrong Answer Journal nothing\\n- 7:43If you blow off Wrong Answer Journaling for a time and then you start to build up a debt honestly, just write off those past sections don\'t Wrong Answer Journal them and try and start fresh of Okay, the ones I do today though I\'m gonna Wrong Answer Journal those because very often the debt\\n- 7:57of having so many in the past a Wrong Answer Journal from the far Past will keep people from doing it at all because they know they could never get out of debt And it\'s also true that your sections from like three weeks ago That you got wrong and stuff like that those wrong answers are not\\n- 8:11as valuable to Wrong Answer Journal at this stage You would get a lot more return on your investment from Wrong Answer Journaling things that happened much much more recently like things to Camo today\\n- 8:20And so I hope that that helps. I hope that tells you how to Camo and I can\'t wait to see how you do\\n\\n##### In This Training Block\\n\\n### Ellen\'s Notes\\n\\n***Camo Tips***\\n\\nNever check the answer key on any section before Camoing\\n\\nWait 24 hours before Camoing\\n\\nNever press Complete Section in LawHub\\n\\nDo your Wrong Answer Journal when you Camo\\n\\n### Take Notes\\n\\n## Video Course Review\\n\\n[\\n\\nConcept Review\\n\\n](https://new.theloophole.com/training-camp/concept-review)\\r\\n\\n### FILE: C:\\\\Users\\\\Joshu\\\\dev\\\\ai-os\\\\raw\\\\sources\\\\old-obsidian-vault-2026-10-07\\\\Clippings\\\\loophole-online\\\\training-camp\\\\concept-review\\\\How and Why to Do Decaf RC Translation • LO.md\\n\\r\\n---\\ntitle: \\"How and Why to Do Decaf RC Translation • LO\\"\\nsource: \\"https://new.theloophole.com/training-camp/browse/concept-review/rc-translation-strategy/how-and-why-to-do-decaf-rc-translation\\"\\nauthor:\\npublished:\\ncreated: 2026-08-30\\ndescription: \\"LSAT prep from Ellen Cassidy, author of The Loophole in LSAT Logical Reasoning — video lessons, drills, and guided study plans.\\"\\ntags:\\n  - \\"clippings\\"\\n---\\n### Ellen\'s Notes\\n\\n***Decaf RC Translation Drill***\\n\\n**NOTE**: If you want to do your recaps out loud in the beginning to keep yourself honest about the quality and completeness of the recaps, that\'s totally fine. Transition to silent recaps when you feel like you\'re ready.\\n\\n1. Read, translate, and quip paragraph 1. Do it just like LR.\\n\\t1. This means all the details are included just like they would be on LR.\\n\\t\\t2. Quip after *every* Translation.\\n\\t\\t3. This standard applies to every paragraph you ever translate on RC.\\n2. Look away from paragraph 1 and recap the **FULL TRANSLATION** of paragraph 1 silently.\\n\\t1. The recap is not a summary, gist, or main point.\\n\\t\\t2. The recap should not omit names, dates, and lists.\\n\\t\\t3. The recap should not be a shopping list of information. It should be a story in your own words.\\n\\t\\t4. It is encouraged to mix up the order of the sentences and ideas.\\n\\t\\t5. This standard applies to every paragraph (or collection of paragraphs) you ever recap on RC.\\n3. Read, translate, and quip paragraph 2. Do it just like LR.\\n4. Look away from paragraph 2 and recap the **FULL TRANSLATION** of paragraph 2 silently.\\n5. Without looking back, recap the Translations of paragraphs 1 + 2 back to back silently.\\n6. Read, translate, a…35906 tokens truncated…recap should not be a shopping list of information. It should be a story in your own words.\\n\\t\\t4. It is encouraged to mix up the order of the sentences and ideas.\\n\\t\\t5. This standard applies to every paragraph (or collection of paragraphs) you ever recap on RC.\\n3. Read, translate, and quip paragraph 2. Do it just like LR.\\n4. Look away from paragraph 2 and recap the **FULL TRANSLATION** of paragraph 2 silently.\\n5. Without looking back, recap the Translations of paragraphs 1 + 2 back to back silently.\\n6. Read, translate, and quip paragraph 3. Do it just like LR.\\n7. Look away from paragraph 3 and recap the **FULL TRANSLATION** of paragraph 3 silently.\\n8. Without looking back, recap the Translations of paragraphs 1 + 2 + 3 back to back silently.\\n9. Repeat this pattern for all remaining paragraphs.\\n10. Complete this same process on all remaining passages in the section.\\n\\nvideo walkthrough\\n\\n[How and Why to Do Decaf RC Translation](https://new.theloophole.com/training-camp/browse/stage/translation/video/how-and-why-to-do-decaf-rc-translation)\\n\\nSections to use\\n\\nMix in Previously Seen Sections\\n\\nhow to time\\n\\nCount-Up Stopwatch\\n\\nper passage time goal to move on\\n\\n12 min (outloud recaps), 8 min (silent recaps)\\n\\n## Ellen\'s Advice\\n\\n### If You Want to Win\\n\\n- If you have a 70-minute time accommodation, you can use Decaf RC Translation on test day. You do not have to master Cappuccino RC Translation and Caffeinated RC Translation for use on test day, if Decaf RC Translation offers superior Translation retention.\\n\\n## The Expanded Universe\\n\\n### Videos to Supercharge Your Workout\\n\\n### Get Help in These Training Blocks\\n\\n1\\n\\n### Turn This Workout Into a Full Routine\\n\\nQuick Routine, 3 Workouts\\n\\n### [The RC Working Memory Workout Routine](https://new.theloophole.com/workouts-and-routines/routines/the-rc-working-memory-workout-routine)\\n\\nYou. will. remember. the. passage.\\n\\n🧱 Daily Foundation\\n\\nLR\\n\\nRC\\n\\nQuick Routine, 6 Workouts\\n\\n### [AC Primer + Decaf Routine](https://new.theloophole.com/workouts-and-routines/routines/ac-primer-plus-decaf-routine)\\n\\nRemember the passage and answer choices for real\\n\\n🧱 Daily Foundation\\n\\nLR\\n\\nRC\\n\\nRoutine, 6 Workouts\\n\\n### [Decaf + LR Sections Routine](https://new.theloophole.com/workouts-and-routines/routines/decaf-plus-lr-sections-routine)\\n\\nBuild the rock solid foundation your goal score needs\\n\\n🧱 Daily Foundation\\n\\nMost Repeated\\n\\nTop Performing\\n\\nLR\\n\\nRC\\n\\nQuick Routine, 3 Workouts\\n\\n### [The RC Get to Know You Routine](https://new.theloophole.com/workouts-and-routines/routines/the-rc-get-to-know-you-routine)\\n\\nRC can be breezy and light\\n\\n🧱 Daily Foundation\\n\\nRC\\n\\nRoutine, 5 Workouts\\n\\n### [Non-Native Speaker Advanced Beginner Translation Routine](https://new.theloophole.com/workouts-and-routines/routines/non-native-speaker-advanced-beginner-translation-routine)\\n\\nStay patient with yourself; you\'re doing something great here\\n\\n🧱 Daily Foundation\\n\\nLR\\n\\nRC\\n\\nRoutine, 5 Workouts\\n\\n### [Translation + Decaf Routine](https://new.theloophole.com/workouts-and-routines/routines/translation-plus-decaf-routine)\\n\\nThis might be the hardest Routine you do in Loophole Online\\n\\n🧱 Daily Foundation\\n\\nCommunity Favorite\\n\\nMost Repeated\\n\\nLR\\n\\nRC\\r\\n\\n### FILE: C:\\\\Users\\\\Joshu\\\\dev\\\\ai-os\\\\raw\\\\sources\\\\old-obsidian-vault-2026-10-07\\\\Clippings\\\\loophole-online\\\\workouts\\\\Method Flaw Argument Part Answer Choice Translation Spreadsheet • LO.md\\n\\r\\n---\\ntitle: \\"Method Flaw Argument Part Answer Choice Translation Spreadsheet • LO\\"\\nsource: \\"https://new.theloophole.com/workouts-and-routines/workouts/method-flaw-argument-part-answer-choice-translation-spreadsheet\\"\\nauthor:\\npublished:\\ncreated: 2026-08-30\\ndescription: \\"LSAT prep from Ellen Cassidy, author of The Loophole in LSAT Logical Reasoning — video lessons, drills, and guided study plans.\\"\\ntags:\\n  - \\"clippings\\"\\n---\\n## Instructions\\n\\n1. Make a spreadsheet with three columns, or use [this template](https://docs.google.com/spreadsheets/d/1V7BS4BgrznJf_JQepzRK07dJw5yX0dZijLFSFYDqank/edit?usp=sharing):\\n\\t1. **Column 1**: Vexing Answer Choice\\n\\t\\t2. **Column 2**: Answer Choice Translation\\n\\t\\t3. **Column 3**: Corresponding Stimulus\\n2. Collect all the difficult answer choices to translate, focusing especially on Method/Flaw/Argument Part answer choices.\\n\\t1. This could include harvesting answer choices from your sections immediately after you\'re done or going back over old sections to harvest answer choices.\\n3. Write the difficult answers in the Vexing Answer Choice column of the spreadsheet, one answer choice per row.\\n4. Translate the answers in the Answer Choice Translation column of the spreadsheet.\\n5. In the Corresponding Stimulus column of the spreadsheet, write an example stimulus which would make the answer choice in that row correct.\\r\\n\\n### FILE: C:\\\\Users\\\\Joshu\\\\dev\\\\ai-os\\\\raw\\\\sources\\\\old-obsidian-vault-2026-10-07\\\\Clippings\\\\loophole-online\\\\workouts\\\\Proto Stimulus Rebound • LO.md\\n\\r\\n---\\ntitle: \\"Proto Stimulus Rebound • LO\\"\\nsource: \\"https://new.theloophole.com/workouts-and-routines/workouts/proto-stimulus-rebound\\"\\nauthor:\\npublished:\\ncreated: 2026-08-30\\ndescription: \\"LSAT prep from Ellen Cassidy, author of The Loophole in LSAT Logical Reasoning — video lessons, drills, and guided study plans.\\"\\ntags:\\n  - \\"clippings\\"\\n---\\n## Quick Facts\\n\\n### Will This Help Me?\\n\\nThis will help you if\\n\\nYou forget a significant amount of the stimulus by the time you finish the CLIR.\\n\\nSkip for now if\\n\\nYou retain the all information in the stimulus after you\'ve finished the CLIR.\\n\\nchallenge indexBuckle Up\\n\\nlevelBeginner\\n\\nminimum time25+ minutes\\n\\n![clock](https://new.theloophole.com/_next/static/media/clock.f4c45ff9.svg)\\n\\ntiming compatible withStopwatch\\n\\n## Instructions\\n\\n**NOTE**: Proto-Stimulus Rebounds may be done with or without answering the question.\\n\\n1. Translate, quip, and CLIR the stimulus\\\\*.\\n2. Cover the stimulus and say the full Translation from memory\\n\\t1. Try to reorder the argument parts in your retelling of the Translation.\\n3. Relook at the stimulus and see if anything is missing, added, or changed.\\n\\t1. Take a note of what was missing, added, or changed.\\n4. Complete the rest of the question, if you choose to do so.\\n\\nSections to use\\n\\nMix in Previously Seen Sections\\n\\nhow to time\\n\\nCount-Up Stopwatch\\n\\n## The Expanded Universe\\n\\n### Similar Workouts You\'ll Probably Enjoy\\n\\nWorkout\\n\\n### [Translation + CLIR Drill](https://new.theloophole.com/workouts-and-routines/workouts/translation-plus-clir-drill)\\n\\nTransform your reading for LSAT mastery\\n\\nMost Popular\\n\\nMost Repeated\\n\\nMost Improved\\n\\nLR\\n\\n### Turn This Workout Into a Full Routine\\n\\nRoutine, 6 Workouts\\n\\n### [Non-Native Speaker Intermediate Translation Routine](https://new.theloophole.com/workouts-and-routines/routines/non-native-speaker-intermediate-translation-routine)\\n\\nYou can seriously improve your English skills\\n\\n🧱 Daily Foundation\\n\\nLR\\n\\nRC\\r\\n\\n### FILE: C:\\\\Users\\\\Joshu\\\\dev\\\\ai-os\\\\raw\\\\sources\\\\old-obsidian-vault-2026-10-07\\\\Clippings\\\\loophole-online\\\\workouts\\\\Translation Marathon • LO.md\\n\\r\\n---\\ntitle: \\"Translation Marathon • LO\\"\\nsource: \\"https://new.theloophole.com/workouts-and-routines/workouts/translation-marathon\\"\\nauthor:\\npublished:\\ncreated: 2026-08-30\\ndescription: \\"LSAT prep from Ellen Cassidy, author of The Loophole in LSAT Logical Reasoning — video lessons, drills, and guided study plans.\\"\\ntags:\\n  - \\"clippings\\"\\n---\\n## Quick Facts\\n\\n### Will This Help Me?\\n\\nThis will help you if\\n\\nYou need to bring your Translation time down to meet the Translation time goals.\\n\\nSkip for now if\\n\\nYou are past the Translation stage of prep.\\n\\nchallenge indexEllen Whyyy\\n\\nlevelBeginner\\n\\nminimum time120+ minutes\\n\\n![clock](https://new.theloophole.com/_next/static/media/clock.f4c45ff9.svg)\\n\\ntiming compatible withStopwatch\\n\\n## Instructions\\n\\n1. Complete six to eight Translation + CLIR Drills\\\\* in one sitting with no breaks.\\n\\nSections to use\\n\\nNew Sections Only\\n\\nhow to time\\n\\nCount-Up Stopwatch\\n\\n## Ellen\'s Advice\\n\\n### If You Want to Win\\n\\n- **It is very important that you do not take breaks between sections or check your sections in the middle of the Marathon.**\\n\\t- Marathons only work because your brain doesn’t get the chance to re-adapt to non-LSAT thinking.\\n\\t\\t- Taking breaks guarantees that the Marathon will not work.\\n\\t\\t- Do not check your phone.\\n\\t\\t\\t\\t- Do not have conversations.\\n\\t\\t\\t\\t- Do not take a lunch break.\\n\\t\\t- Put snacks and drinks on the desk to eat while you Marathon, if needed.\\n\\t\\t- The only breaks allowed during Translation Marathons are medically required or biologically required breaks.\\n\\t\\t- Running to the bathroom is allowed between sections.\\n\\t\\t\\t\\t- Accommodated breaks for medically required tasks (like insulin injections, etc) are obviously allowed.\\n- Open all the sections you plan to complete in separate tabs before starting the Translation Marathon.\\n- Keep track of the order of the sections in your Translation Marathon, so you can analyze the results.\\n- If you feel yourself getting worse as the Translation Marathon progresses, you are likely becoming fatigued.\\n\\t- This is not cause for alarm. Fatigue is part of the process; it indicates that you need to build stamina.\\n- At the end of the Translation Marathon, take a break and then check all the sections against the CLIR Answer Key in a big batch.\\n\\n### Timing Tips\\n\\n- Set a section timer for 400 minutes in LawHub\'s Self-Paced Mode. **Do not use Exam Mode.**\\n- Time each Translation + CLIR Drill with an external stopwatch. Record the time each drill takes in your tracker.\\n\\n## The Expanded Universe\\n\\n### Videos to Supercharge Your Workout\\n\\n### Get Help in These Training Blocks\\n\\n![How to Translate in 20 Minutes](https://new.theloophole.com/_next/image?url=https%3A%2F%2Fvz-3500a044-c45.b-cdn.net%2Fbcdn_token%3DHS256-pRt1zt2ihN3B46jcEW5gdBaM5t-ai0GnFLSvCSEvoZ0%26token_path%3D%252F6dbdf370-9b76-425c-9547-58127fbea4e7%252F%26expires%3D1788739200%2F6dbdf370-9b76-425c-9547-58127fbea4e7%2Fthumbnail_8a0fe291.jpg&w=3840&q=75)\\n\\n### How to Translate in 20 Minutes\\n\\n25 of 25 Videos Watched\\n\\n25 videos22 min\\n\\n### Similar Workouts You\'ll Probably Enjoy\\n\\nWorkout\\n\\n### [Translation + CLIR Drill](https://new.theloophole.com/workouts-and-routines/workouts/translation-plus-clir-drill)\\n\\nTransform your reading for LSAT mastery\\n\\nMost Popular\\n\\nMost Repeated\\n\\nMost Improved\\n\\nLR\\n\\n### Turn This Workout Into a Full Routine\\n\\nRoutine, 4 Workouts\\n\\n### [Translation Foundation Routine](https://new.theloophole.com/workouts-and-routines/routines/translation-foundation-routine)\\n\\nThe most important Routine in Loophole Online\\n\\n🧱 Daily Foundation\\n\\nCommunity Favorite\\n\\nTop Performing\\n\\nLR\\n\\nRC\\n\\nRoutine, 5 Workouts\\n\\n### [Translation + Decaf Routine](https://new.theloophole.com/workouts-and-routines/routines/translation-plus-decaf-routine)\\n\\nThis might be the hardest Routine you do in Loophole Online\\n\\n🧱 Daily Foundation\\n\\nCommunity Favorite\\n\\nMost Repeated\\n\\nLR\\n\\nRC\\n\\n### Helps With These Prep Map Points### Foundation Translation Routine\\n\\nTranslation - Routine 1\\n\\n[View original](https://new.theloophole.com/prep-map/translation/foundation-translation-routine)\\n\\n[\\n\\nTranslation - Routine 2\\n\\n](https://new.theloophole.com/prep-map/translation/translation-plus-decaf-routine)\\r\\n\\n### FILE: C:\\\\Users\\\\Joshu\\\\dev\\\\ai-os\\\\raw\\\\sources\\\\old-obsidian-vault-2026-10-07\\\\Clippings\\\\loophole-online\\\\workouts\\\\Unless Practice Routine • LO.md\\n\\r\\n---\\ntitle: \\"Unless Practice Routine • LO\\"\\nsource: \\"https://new.theloophole.com/workouts-and-routines/workouts/unless-practice-routine\\"\\nauthor:\\npublished:\\ncreated: 2026-08-30\\ndescription: \\"LSAT prep from Ellen Cassidy, author of The Loophole in LSAT Logical Reasoning — video lessons, drills, and guided study plans.\\"\\ntags:\\n  - \\"clippings\\"\\n---\\n## Quick Facts\\n\\n### Will This Help Me?\\n\\nThis will help you if\\n\\nYou take longer than 5 seconds to diagram Unless or you make mistakes diagramming Unless greater than 1% of the time\\n\\nSkip for now if\\n\\nYou diagram Unless automatically in less than 5 seconds 100% of the time\\n\\nchallenge indexPretty Chill\\n\\nlevelBeginner\\n\\nminimum time3+ minutes\\n\\n![clock](https://new.theloophole.com/_next/static/media/clock.f4c45ff9.svg)\\n\\ntiming compatible withStopwatch\\n\\n## Instructions\\n\\n1. Diagram three out of these four Unless statements:\\n\\t1. ~A, unless B.\\n\\t\\t2. Unless B, A.\\n\\t\\t3. Unless ~B, ~A.\\n\\t\\t4. A, unless ~B.\\n2. Switch up which three statements you diagram every time.\\n3. Start every LSAT study session with this quick workout until you can diagram Unless accurately in under 5 seconds.\\n\\nSections to use\\n\\nDoesn\'t Use a Section\\n\\nhow to time\\n\\nCount-Up Stopwatch\\n\\ntime goal to move on\\n\\n15 sec\\n\\n## Ellen\'s Advice\\n\\n### If You Want to Win\\n\\n- Don\'t simply memorize the correct diagrams. Diagram each statement fresh every time.\\n- Change the letters in the statements.\\n- You\'re working too hard to lose points on your real LSAT because you didn\'t really get Unless.\\n\\n### Timing Tips\\n\\n- It shouldn\'t take you more than five seconds to diagram an individual Unless statement once you really understand how Unless works.\\n- The entire Routine shouldn\'t take you more than 15 seconds total once you\'re trained\\n\\t- I just diagrammed three Unless statements in 9 seconds to prove it can be done.\\n- If the Unless Practice Routine takes you much longer than 15 seconds, it just shows how much you need to do it\\n- Once you hit the point where you can diagram three Unless statements in 15 seconds, you can lower your Unless Practice Routine frequency to once per week.\\n\\n## Real Examples\\n\\n***Unless Practice Routine Answer Key***\\r\\n\\n### FILE: C:\\\\Users\\\\Joshu\\\\dev\\\\ai-os\\\\raw\\\\sources\\\\old-obsidian-vault-2026-10-07\\\\Clippings\\\\loophole-online\\\\workouts\\\\Wrong Answer Journal • LO.md\\n\\r\\n---\\ntitle: \\"Wrong Answer Journal • LO\\"\\nsource: \\"https://new.theloophole.com/workouts-and-routines/workouts/wrong-answer-journal\\"\\nauthor:\\npublished:\\ncreated: 2026-08-30\\ndescription: \\"LSAT prep from Ellen Cassidy, author of The Loophole in LSAT Logical Reasoning — video lessons, drills, and guided study plans.\\"\\ntags:\\n  - \\"clippings\\"\\n---\\n## Quick Facts\\n\\n### Will This Help Me?\\n\\nThis will help you if\\n\\nYou need to improve your accuracy and stop making the same mistakes over and over again.\\n\\nSkip for now if\\n\\nWrong Answer Journals are harmless.\\n\\nchallenge indexPretty Chill\\n\\nlevelBeginner\\n\\nminimum time15+ minutes\\n\\n![clock](https://new.theloophole.com/_next/static/media/clock.f4c45ff9.svg)\\n\\ntiming compatible withNot Timed\\n\\n## Instructions\\n\\n1. After you\'ve Camoed your LR or RC section, begin the Wrong Answer Journaling process.\\n2. For each wrong answer, fill in the date you did the section, the question number that was wrong, and the answer you chose.\\n\\t1. This information will already be filled in for you if you Camo using Loophole Online.\\n3. Fill in your Reason Missed column\\n\\t1. In this column, write down **what you did wrong that caused you to miss this question**.\\n\\t\\t1. Ask yourself, \\"Why did I think that this wrong answer was right? Why did I think that this right answer was wrong?\\" Focus on changing the actions that enabled these mistaken judgments.\\n\\t\\t2. The first word of every entry in this column should be \\"I.\\"\\n\\t\\t1. Example: \\"I misidentified the question stem.\\"\\n\\t\\t\\t\\t2. This will force you to reflect on **what you did** that caused you to choose the wrong answer and overlook the right answer.\\n4. Fill in your Solution column\\n\\t1. In this column, write down **what you will do differently to ensure you don\'t make this same mistake again**.\\n\\t\\t1. Ask yourself, \\"What could I have done that would have made me realize in the moment that the wrong answer is wrong? What could I have done that would have made me realize in the moment that the right answer is right?\\"\\n\\t\\t2. The first words of every entry in this column should be \\"I will...\\"\\n\\t\\t1. Example: \\"I will do Question Stem Training to improve my ability to correctly identify question types.\\"\\n\\t\\t3. Frame your Solution Column as a direct response to the Reason Missed column.\\n\\nClick [here](https://new.theloophole.com/review-and-camo/wrong-answer-journal) to go to your Wrong Answer Journal\\n\\nvideo walkthrough\\n\\n[How to Use the Wrong Answer Journal](https://new.theloophole.com/training-camp/browse/stage/accuracy/video/how-to-use-the-wrong-answer-journal)\\n\\nSections to use\\n\\nPreviously Seen Sections Only\\n\\nhow to time\\n\\nUntimed\\n\\n## Ellen\'s Advice\\n\\n### If You Want to Win\\n\\n- Keep your Wrong Answer Journal entries short and sweet. One to two sentences is enough for each column.\\n- **Do not waste time in your Wrong Answer Journal writing down detailed explanations of why the answer you chose was wrong, and why the right answer is right in the exact context of the question.**\\n\\t- If you\'re writing anything that cannot be ported directly to subsequent sections, you are wasting your Wrong Answer Journal time.\\n- The Wrong Answer Journal gives you a chance to identify the exact **actions you took** that made you make the wrong decision. It then gives you the opportunity to decide which actions you will take in the future that will enable you to make correct decisions. This is the essence of improvement on the LSAT.\\n\\t- I want you to know why an answer is right and another is wrong, but I don\'t want you to stop there. I want you to start there.\\n\\t\\t- After you know why the correct answer is correct and why the wrong answer is wrong, look more deeply at your actions and mindset in relation to the question.\\n\\t\\t- What did you do that caused this result? And what will you do to ensure a different result next time?\\n- Do not rely on cheap explanations in your Reason Missed column like \\"silly mistake,\\" \\"misread,\\" or \\"rushing.\\"\\n\\t- Dive into why you made this silly mistake, why you misread, and why you were rushing.\\n\\t\\t- This is how you can identify the problematic actions that lead to problematic judgments and change them for the better.\\n\\n## The Expanded Universe\\n\\n### Videos to Supercharge Your Workout\\n\\n### Get Help in These Training Blocks\\n\\n1\\n\\n### Similar Workouts You\'ll Probably Enjoy\\n\\nQuick Workout\\n\\n### [Camo Review](https://new.theloophole.com/workouts-and-routines/workouts/camo-review)\\n\\nDouble your improvement from every section\\n\\nMost Improved\\n\\nLR\\n\\nRC\\n\\n### Turn This Workout Into a Full Routine\\n\\nRoutine, 5 Workouts\\n\\n### [Failsafe Marathon Routine](https://new.theloophole.com/workouts-and-routines/routines/failsafe-marathon-routine)\\n\\nThis is your Speed in-case-of-emergency button\\n\\n🌿 Spaced Set\\n\\nLR\\n\\nRC\\n\\nQuick Routine, 5 Workouts\\n\\n### [Timing Failsafe Routine](https://new.theloophole.com/workouts-and-routines/routines/timing-failsafe-routine)\\n\\nSpeed up while keeping your accuracy stable\\n\\n🌿 Spaced Set\\n\\nLR\\n\\nRC\\n\\nRoutine, 2 Workouts\\n\\n### [Weekly Camo + Wrong Answer Journal Routine](https://new.theloophole.com/workouts-and-routines/routines/weekly-camo-plus-wrong-answer-journal-routine)\\n\\nEvery wrong answer raises tomorrow\'s score\\n\\n🔂 Weekly Cycle\\n\\nCommunity Favorite\\n\\nMost Repeated\\n\\nTop Performing\\n\\nLR\\n\\nRC\\n\\nRoutine, 5 Workouts\\n\\n### [Marathon Routine](https://new.theloophole.com/workouts-and-routines/routines/marathon-routine)\\n\\nYou\'ll do this if you really want to bring your time down\\n\\n🌿 Spaced Set\\n\\nCommunity Favorite\\n\\nLR\\n\\nRC\\n\\nRoutine, 5 Workouts\\n\\n### [Mixed Section Marathon Routine](https://new.theloophole.com/workouts-and-routines/routines/mixed-section-marathon-routine)\\n\\nLike a PT on steroids\\n\\n🌿 Spaced Set\\n\\nLR\\n\\nRC\\r\\n"}'}]
